PERSONAL PROTOTYPE · INDEPENDENT REVIEW PENDING

The Book of Prayer.

Complete numbered reports 342–453. Available chapter titles use provisional English. Disputed headings are labelled under review; unnumbered chapter introductions remain separately preserved in Arabic.

Edition comparison history

These notes record findings as successive batches were prepared. Later entries may resolve earlier holds. Use the individual entry notes below for the evidence supplied with the current reading text.

Selected Arabic wording is unchanged. Bewley divides the opening report and places the following Aisha account under a shared reference; both scopes are explicit. Its separate Clothing in the Prayer section is not treated as a new Arabic book. The printed chapter 1 title differs in singular/plural form between witnesses, and Bewley’s introductory wording differs from the selected والصدق. Introductions remain Arabic and outside daily sessions. Ambiguous lexical choices and print/English omissions are recorded; this AI-assisted personal prototype is not independently certified. Hadith 342

AI-assisted working translation. Independent review pending.

Open this hadith →

Comparison findings

  • The selected Arabic is one report. Bewley divides its material across 342 and the first account under 343; an unnumbered Aisha account under that same 343 corresponds to selected 343. Both scopes are recorded explicitly. The Khan-family EPUB 1.8.345 has the main narrative but omits the Ibn Shihab / Ibn Hazm / Ibn Abbas and Abu Habbah attribution before the pens sentence. All selected chains and narrator transitions remain.
  • The selected text and modern print have فراجعني; the historical print has فراجعت. The working English retains the former as he sent me back, following the explanation in Mirqat al-Mafatih. Shatr can denote half or a part; the compared English uses half, while Fath al-Bari explicitly discusses part here. This provisional rendering uses part without supplying an arithmetic sequence or importing additional visits from other reports. Independent lexical review is required.
  • The selected and modern wording has حبايل اللؤلؤ, provisionally strings of pearls. The historical print instead has جنابذ اللؤلؤ. Fath al-Bari discusses both readings and competing explanations; the historical domes wording is not substituted into the selected Arabic. The Khan-family comparison also renders this differently. These are recorded variants, not reconciled descriptions.
  • The selected sixth heaven, order of encounters, plural blessing and change from narrated third person to first person remain. Fath al-Bari discusses differing reports; no seventh heaven or reordered encounters are imported. The historical print has additional honorifics and a shorter wording at several points. The selected form remains unchanged.
  • The English expands the source honorific symbols only. It does not import the comparisons’ parenthetical explanations of reward, the identity of the pens’ writers or additional place descriptions. Figures renders أسودة; souls renders نسم. These lexical choices remain provisional.

AI-assisted translation of the complete selected Arabic, compared with two visually inspected Arabic prints and two individually matched English comparison families. Omissions, shared references and wording variants remain documented. Independent Arabic, English, lexical, chapter and reference review is pending; this is not certification.

Complete working English

Yahya ibn Bukayr related to us. He said: Al-Layth related to us, from Yunus, from Ibn Shihab, from Anas ibn Malik, who said: Abu Dharr used to relate that the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

The roof of my house was opened while I was in Makkah. Jibril descended, opened my chest and then washed it with Zamzam water. Then he brought a basin of gold filled with wisdom and faith, poured it into my chest and closed it. Then he took my hand and ascended with me to the lowest heaven.

When I came to the lowest heaven, Jibril said to the keeper of the heaven: “Open.” He said: “Who is this?” He said: “This is Jibril.” He said: “Is anyone with you?” He said: “Yes, Muhammad, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, is with me.” He said: “Has he been sent for?” He said: “Yes.”

When he opened, we ascended into the lowest heaven. There was a man sitting with figures to his right and figures to his left. When he looked towards his right, he laughed, and when he looked towards his left, he wept. He said: “Welcome to the righteous Prophet and the righteous son.” I said to Jibril: “Who is this?” He said: “This is Adam, and these figures to his right and left are the souls of his children. Those on the right are among the people of Paradise, and the figures on his left are the people of the Fire. When he looks to his right, he laughs, and when he looks towards his left, he weeps.” Then he ascended with me to the second heaven and said to its keeper: “Open.” Its keeper said to him the same as the first had said, and he opened.

Anas said: He mentioned that he found Adam, Idris, Musa, ‘Isa and Ibrahim in the heavens—may Allah’s blessings be upon them. He did not specify their positions, except that he mentioned finding Adam in the lowest heaven and Ibrahim in the sixth heaven.

Anas said: When Jibril passed by Idris with the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, he said: “Welcome to the righteous Prophet and the righteous brother.” I said: “Who is this?” He said: “This is Idris.” Then I passed by Musa, and he said: “Welcome to the righteous Prophet and the righteous brother.” I said: “Who is this?” He said: “This is Musa.” Then I passed by ‘Isa, and he said: “Welcome to the righteous brother and the righteous Prophet.” I said: “Who is this?” He said: “This is ‘Isa.” Then I passed by Ibrahim, and he said: “Welcome to the righteous Prophet and the righteous son.” I said: “Who is this?” He said: “This is Ibrahim, may Allah bless him and grant him peace.”

Ibn Shihab said: Then Ibn Hazm informed me that Ibn ‘Abbas and Abu Habbah al-Ansari used to say: The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

Then I was taken up until I reached a height where I could hear the scratching of pens.

Ibn Hazm and Anas ibn Malik said: The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

Allah made fifty prayers obligatory for my community. I returned with that until I passed by Musa, who said: “What has Allah made obligatory for you upon your community?” I said: “He has made fifty prayers obligatory.” He said: “Return to your Lord, for your community cannot bear that.” So he sent me back, and He removed part of them. I returned to Musa and said: “He has removed part of them.” He said: “Go back to your Lord, for your community cannot bear it.” I went back, and He removed part of them. I returned to him, and he said: “Return to your Lord, for your community cannot bear that.” I went back to Him, and He said: “They are five and they are fifty. The word is not changed with Me.” I returned to Musa, and he said: “Go back to your Lord.” I said: “I feel ashamed before my Lord.”

Then I was taken onwards until I reached the Lote-tree of the Utmost Boundary. Colours covered it, and I do not know what they were. Then I was brought into Paradise, and there were strings of pearls in it, and its soil was musk.

Unchanged Arabic report

[342] حدثنا يحيى بن بكير قال: حدثنا الليث عن يونس عن ابن شهاب عن أنس بن مالك قال: كان أبو ذر يحدث أن رسول الله ﷺ قال: فرج عن سقف بيتي وأنا بمكة فنزل جبريل ففرج صدري ثم غسله بماء زمزم ثم جاء بطست من ذهب ممتلئ حكمة وإيمانا فأفرغه في صدري ثم أطبقه ثم أخذ بيدي فعرج بي إلى السماء الدنيا فلما جئت إلى السماء الدنيا قال جبريل لخازن السماء افتح قال من هذا قال هذا جبريل قال هل معك أحد قال: نعم معي محمد ﷺ، فقال أرسل إليه قال: نعم فلما فتح علونا السماء الدنيا فإذا رجل قاعد على يمينه أسودة وعلى يساره أسودة إذا نظر قبل يمينه ضحك وإذا نظر قبل يساره بكى، فقال مرحبا بالنبي الصالح والابن الصالح قلت لجبريل من هذا قال هذا آدم وهذه الأسودة عن يمينه وشماله نسم بنيه فأهل اليمين منهم أهل الجنة والأسودة التي عن شماله أهل النار فإذا نظر عن يمينه ضحك وإذا نظر قبل شماله بكى حتى عرج بي إلى السماء الثانية، فقال لخازنها افتح، فقال له خازنها مثل ما قال الأول ففتح قال أنس فذكر أنه وجد في السماوات آدم وإدريس وموسى وعيسى وإبراهيم صلوات الله عليهم ولم يثبت كيف منازلهم غير أنه ذكر أنه وجد آدم في السماء الدنيا وإبراهيم في السماء السادسة قال أنس فلما مر جبريل بالنبي ﷺ بإدريس قال مرحبا بالنبي الصالح والأخ الصالح فقلت: من هذا قال هذا إدريس ثم مررت بموسى، فقال مرحبا بالنبي الصالح والأخ الصالح قلت: من هذا قال هذا موسى ثم مررت بعيسى، فقال مرحبا بالأخ الصالح والنبي الصالح قلت: من هذا قال هذا عيسى ثم مررت بإبراهيم، فقال مرحبا بالنبي الصالح والابن الصالح قلت: من هذا قال هذا إبراهيم ﷺ قال ابن شهاب فأخبرني ابن حزم ان ابن عباس وأبا حبة الأنصارى كانا يقولان قال النبي ﷺ ثم عرج بي حتى ظهرت لمستوى أسمع فيه صريف الأقلام قال ابن حزم وأنس بن مالك قال النبي ﷺ ففرض الله على أمتي خمسين صلاة فرجعت بذلك حتى مررت على موسى، فقال: ما فرض الله لك على أمتك قلت: فرض خمسين صلاة قال: فارجع إلى ربك فإن أمتك لا تطيق ذلك فراجعني فوضع شطرها فرجعت إلى موسى قلت: وضع شطرها، فقال راجع ربك فإن أمتك لا تطيق فراجعت فوضع شطرها فرجعت إليه، فقال ارجع إلى ربك فإن أمتك لا تطيق ذلك فراجعته، فقال هي خمس وهي خمسون لا يبدل القول لدي فرجعت إلى موسى، فقال راجع ربك فقلت: استحييت من ربي ثم انطلق بي حتى انتهى بي إلى سدرة المنتهى وغشيها ألوان لا أدري ما هي ثم أدخلت الجنة فإذا فيها حبايل اللؤلؤ وإذا ترابها المسك

Chapter heading and introduction

How the prayers were made obligatory during the Night Journey

The heading’s English is provisional. Any following introduction is preserved in Arabic; it is separate from the numbered hadith and its English translation remains pending.

باب كيف فرضت الصلوات في الإسراء

وقال ابن عباس حدثني أبو سفيان في حديث هرقل، فقال يأمرنا يعني النبي ﷺ بالصلاة والصدق والعفاف

Hadith 343

AI-assisted working translation. Independent review pending.

Open this hadith →

Comparison findings

  • Both inspected Arabic prints preserve the complete Aisha report and the distinction between travelling and resident prayer. The doubled ركعتين ركعتين is rendered two rakahs for each prayer, following its distributive force discussed in Fath al-Bari. No later four-rakah total, exception for Maghrib or legal conclusion is added.
  • Bewley has this as the unnumbered Aisha paragraph after the Night Journey continuation under 343. Its explicit second-part scope prevents the entire comparison 343 being misassigned to either selected entry. The Khan-family comparison is 1.8.346; the modern Arabic print numbers the report 350.

AI-assisted translation of the complete selected Arabic, compared with two visually inspected Arabic prints and two individually matched English comparison families. Omissions, shared references and wording variants remain documented. Independent Arabic, English, lexical, chapter and reference review is pending; this is not certification.

Complete working English

‘Abd Allah ibn Yusuf related to us. He said: Malik informed us, from Salih ibn Kaysan, from ‘Urwah ibn al-Zubayr, from ‘A’ishah, the Mother of the Believers, who said:

When Allah first made prayer obligatory, He prescribed two rak‘ahs for each prayer, both while resident and while travelling. The travelling prayer was left as it was, and the resident prayer was increased.

Unchanged Arabic report

[343] حدثنا عبد الله بن يوسف قال: أخبرنا مالك عن صالح بن كيسان عن عروة بن الزبير عن عائشة أم المؤمنين قالت فرض الله الصلاة حين فرضها ركعتين ركعتين في الحضر والسفر فأقرت صلاة السفر وزيد في صلاة الحضر

Hadith 344

AI-assisted working translation. Independent review pending.

Open this hadith →

Comparison findings

  • The complete selected closing transmission through Abd Allah ibn Raja, Imran and Muhammad ibn Sirin remains. It appears in the historical print and Bewley, but is absent from the inspected modern print and Khan-family EPUB body. Neither absence deletes it from this reader.
  • دعوتهم is rendered their supplication; Bewley instead uses feasts here. Jilbab is retained as the source garment term rather than being silently reduced to a face veil. The selected singular من جلبابها remains; the historical print has the plural جلابيبها. No names, legal conclusion or additional garment instructions are supplied.

AI-assisted translation of the complete selected Arabic, compared with two visually inspected Arabic prints and two individually matched English comparison families. Omissions, shared references and wording variants remain documented. Independent Arabic, English, lexical, chapter and reference review is pending; this is not certification.

Complete working English

Musa ibn Isma‘il related to us. He said: Yazid ibn Ibrahim related to us, from Muhammad, from Umm ‘Atiyyah, who said:

We were instructed to bring out the menstruating women and the women in seclusion on the days of the two Eids, so that they might attend the gathering of the Muslims and their supplication. The menstruating women were to stay away from their place of prayer. A woman said: “Messenger of Allah, one of us does not have a jilbab.” He said: “Let her companion clothe her from her own jilbab.”

And ‘Abd Allah ibn Raja’ said: ‘Imran related to us; Muhammad ibn Sirin related to us; Umm ‘Atiyyah related to us: “I heard this from the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace.”

The obligation of wearing clothes in prayer

The heading’s English is provisional. Any following introduction is preserved in Arabic; it is separate from the numbered hadith and its English translation remains pending.

باب وجوب الصلاة في الثياب

وقول الله تعالى { خذوا زينتكم عند كل مسجد } ومن صلى ملتحفا في ثوب واحد ويذكر عن سلمة بن الأكوع أن النبي ﷺ قال: يزره ولو بشوكة في إسناده نظر ومن صلى في الثوب الذي يجامع فيه ما لم ير أذى وأمر النبي ﷺ أن لا يطوف بالبيت عريان

Complete working English

Ahmad ibn Yunus related to us. He said: ‘Asim ibn Muhammad related to us. He said: Waqid ibn Muhammad related to me, from Muhammad ibn al-Munkadir, who said:

Jabir prayed in an izar which he had tied at the back of his neck, while his other clothes were placed on the clothes stand. Someone said to him: “Do you pray in a single izar?” He said: “I did this only so that a fool like you would see me. Which of us had two garments in the time of the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace?”

Hadith 350

AI-assisted working translation. Independent review pending.

Open this hadith →

Comparison findings

  • Both stated mawla relationships, the repeated full name Umm Hani bint Abi Talib, the narrator transition she said, eight rakahs and the final forenoon statement remain. Mawla stays a source term rather than assigning a legal relationship not expressed in this sentence.
  • The source says the year of the conquest, not an added place name. My mother’s son retains بن أمي without supplying Ali or an individual identity for so-and-so son of Hubayrah. The two printed witnesses have ابن أمي in that position; the shorter digital spelling بن أمي is retained without a silent character edit. The full repeated protection statement remains.

AI-assisted translation of the complete selected Arabic, compared with two visually inspected Arabic prints and two individually matched English comparison families. Omissions, shared references and wording variants remain documented. Independent Arabic, English, lexical, chapter and reference review is pending; this is not certification.

Complete working English

Isma‘il ibn Abi Uways related to us. He said: Malik ibn Anas related to me, from Abu al-Nadr, the mawla of ‘Umar ibn ‘Ubayd Allah, that Abu Murrah, the mawla of Umm Hani’ bint Abi Talib, informed him that he heard Umm Hani’ bint Abi Talib say:

I went to the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, in the year of the conquest. I found him bathing while his daughter Fatimah screened him. She said: I greeted him, and he said: “Who is this?” I said: “I am Umm Hani’ bint Abi Talib.” He said: “Welcome to Umm Hani’.” When he had finished bathing, he stood and prayed eight rak‘ahs, wrapped in a single garment.

When he finished, I said: “Messenger of Allah, my mother’s son claims that he will kill a man to whom I have granted protection, so-and-so the son of Hubayrah.” The Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said: “We have granted protection to the one to whom you have granted protection, Umm Hani’.” Umm Hani’ said: That was in the forenoon.

Unchanged Arabic report

[350] حدثنا إسماعيل بن أبي أويس قال: حدثني مالك بن أنس عن أبي النضر مولى عمر بن عبيد الله أن أبا مرة مولى أم هانئ بنت أبي طالب أخبره أنه سمع أم هانئ بنت أبي طالب تقول ذهبت إلى رسول الله ﷺ عام الفتح فوجدته يغتسل وفاطمة ابنته تستره قالت فسلمت عليه، فقال من هذه فقلت: أنا أم هانئ بنت أبي طالب، فقال مرحبا بأم هانئ فلما فرغ من غسله قام فصلى ثماني ركعات ملتحفا في ثوب واحد فلما انصرف قلت: يا رسول الله زعم بن أمي أنه قاتل رجلا قد أجرته فلان بن هبيرة، فقال رسول الله ﷺ: قد أجرنا من أجرت يا أم هانئ قالت أم هانئ وذاك ضحى

Complete working English

‘Abd Allah ibn Yusuf related to us. He said: Malik informed us, from Ibn Shihab, from Sa‘id ibn al-Musayyab, from Abu Hurayrah:

That someone asked the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, about praying in a single garment. The Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said: “Do all of you have two garments?”

Hadith 353

AI-assisted working translation. Independent review pending.

Open this hadith →

Comparison findings

  • The full chain retains سمعته أو كنت سألته, the uncertainty between hearing directly and having asked. Fath al-Bari identifies this as Yahya speaking about Ikrimah, not Ikrimah doubting whether he heard Abu Hurayrah. The English parenthesis belongs to that transmission. Neither the alternative wrote to me from other recensions nor an omitted certainty is substituted.
  • Both prints retain the uncertainty and Abu Hurayrah’s explicit testimony. Bewley and the Khan-family comparison abbreviate the earlier chain; the Khan-family body also omits the testimony. The instruction crosses the two ends; on his shoulders is not added from other recensions.

Complete working English

Abu Nu‘aym related to us. He said: Shayban related to us, from Yahya ibn Abi Kathir, from ‘Ikrimah—he said, “I heard him, or I had asked him”—who said: I heard Abu Hurayrah say:

I testify that I heard the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, say: “Whoever prays in a single garment should cross its two ends.”

Hadith 354

AI-assisted working translation. Independent review pending.

Open this hadith →

Comparison findings

  • The selected and both printed witnesses say سألنا (we asked); the Khan-family comparison says I asked. The night-time force of السرى and the narrator’s يعني ضاق explanation remain. Fath al-Bari discusses the abbreviated كان ثوب phrase; the English retains it and its explanation separately rather than silently replacing the quotation.
  • Izar is the waist-wrapped garment, retained as the source term consistently with earlier Prayer reports. No named expedition, errand or additional posture detail from other versions is inserted.

AI-assisted translation of the complete selected Arabic, compared with two visually inspected Arabic prints and two individually matched English comparison families. Omissions, shared references and wording variants remain documented. Independent Arabic, English, lexical, chapter and reference review is pending; this is not certification.

Complete working English

Yahya ibn Salih related to us. He said: Fulayh ibn Sulayman related to us, from Sa‘id ibn al-Harith, who said:

We asked Jabir ibn ‘Abd Allah about praying in a single garment. He said: I went out with the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, on one of his journeys. One night I came on some business of mine and found him praying. I was wearing a single garment, so I wrapped myself in it and prayed beside him.

When he finished, he said: “What brings you out at night, Jabir?” I told him what I needed. When I had finished, he said: “What was this wrapping that I saw?” I said: “It was a garment”—meaning it was too narrow. He said: “If it is wide, wrap yourself in it; if it is narrow, wear it as an izar.”

Hadith 355

Comparison findings

  • The selected and historical print have the passive ويقال للنساء (the women were told); the modern print has وقال للنساء.   • The selected Sahl is not expanded to Sahl ibn Sad, although the historical print gives that longer name. Their necks, the comparison with children and the condition until the men sit upright remain; no explanation of the prohibition is appended.

Complete working English

Musaddad related to us. He said: Yahya related to us, from Sufyan, who said: Abu Hazim related to me, from Sahl, who said:

Men used to pray with the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, with their izars tied around their necks like children. The women were told: “Do not raise your heads until the men are sitting upright.”

Hadith 356

Open this hadith →

Comparison findings

  • Both Arabic prints retain the complete chain, Syrian jubbah, hand in the singular, narrow sleeve, hand brought out from below, ablution, wiping and prayer. The Khan-family comparison uses plural hands. The selected singular remains. Robe is a provisional English garment rendering; leather socks follows the existing reader terminology.

AI-assisted translation of the complete selected Arabic, compared with two visually inspected Arabic prints and two individually matched English comparison families. Omissions, shared references and wording variants remain documented. Independent Arabic, English, lexical, chapter and reference review is pending; this is not certification.

Complete working English

Yahya related to us. He said: Abu Mu‘awiyah related to us, from al-A‘mash, from Muslim, from Masruq, from al-Mughirah ibn Shu‘bah, who said:

I was with the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, on a journey. He said: “Mughirah, take the water vessel.” I took it. The Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, went away until he was out of my sight and relieved himself. He was wearing a Syrian robe. He tried to bring his hand out of its sleeve, but it was too tight, so he brought his hand out from underneath it. I poured water for him, and he performed ablution as he did for prayer, wiped over his leather socks, and then prayed.

  • The complete Matar / Rawh / Zakariyya ibn Ishaq / Amr ibn Dinar / Jabir chain, nephew address, garment beneath the stones, fainting and final honorific remain. Complete working English

Matar ibn al-Fadl related to us. He said: Rawh related to us. He said: Zakariyya ibn Ishaq related to us; ‘Amr ibn Dinar related to us. He said: I heard Jabir ibn ‘Abd Allah relate:

That the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, was carrying stones with them for the Ka‘bah while wearing his izar. His uncle al-‘Abbas said to him: “Nephew, why not loosen your izar and put it on your shoulders beneath the stones?” He said: He loosened it and put it on his shoulders, then fell unconscious. He was not seen naked after that, may Allah bless him and grant him peace.

Hadith 358

AI-assisted working translation. Independent review pending.

Open this hadith →

Comparison findings

  • The clothing statement remains explicitly attributed to Umar, separate from the Prophetic answer. All eight stated pairs and the ninth uncertain pair remain in source order. The final I think he said is not converted into a certain ninth instruction. Fath al-Bari explains the past-tense forms as instructions; that is the provisional English rendering here.
  • Source terms are retained to avoid conflating historical garments: izar is a waist wrap, rida an upper wrap, qaba a coat, and tubban a brief lower garment without trouser legs. Shirt renders qamis; trousers renders sarawil. These descriptions are review notes, not inserted into the quotation. Fath al-Bari distinguishes the garments. No otherwise or additional condition from an English comparison is inserted.

AI-assisted translation of the complete selected Arabic, compared with two visually inspected Arabic prints and two individually matched English comparison families. Omissions, shared references and wording variants remain documented. Independent Arabic, English, lexical, chapter and reference review is pending; this is not certification.

Complete working English

Sulayman ibn Harb related to us. He said: Hammad ibn Zayd related to us, from Ayyub, from Muhammad, from Abu Hurayrah, who said:

A man stood up before the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, and asked him about praying in a single garment. He said: “Can all of you find two garments?”

Then a man asked ‘Umar, who said: “When Allah gives you abundance, make use of it. Let a man gather his garments about him. Let a man pray in an izar and a rida, in an izar and a shirt, in an izar and a qaba, in trousers and a rida, in trousers and a shirt, in trousers and a qaba, in a tubban and a qaba, in a tubban and a shirt.” He said: And I think he said: “In a tubban and a rida.”

Comparison findings

  • The selected report, both prints and Bewley preserve the closing Nafi / Ibn Umar transmission. The matched Khan-family EPUB body omits it; it remains a separate complete chain segment. This is related content to selected 134 but its order and wording are not replaced by that earlier report.

AI-assisted translation of the complete selected Arabic, compared with two visually inspected Arabic prints and two individually matched English comparison families. Omissions, shared references and wording variants remain documented. Independent Arabic, English, lexical, chapter and reference review is pending; this is not certification.

Complete working English

‘Asim ibn ‘Ali related to us. He said: Ibn Abi Dhi’b related to us, from al-Zuhri, from Salim, from Ibn ‘Umar, who said:

A man asked the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace: “What does a person in ihram wear?” He said: “He does not wear a shirt, trousers, a hooded cloak, or a garment touched by saffron or wars. Whoever cannot find sandals should wear leather socks and cut them so that they are below the ankles.”

And from Nafi‘, from Ibn ‘Umar, from the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, the same.

Comparison findings

  • The exact source term samma is retained in the working English. Fath al-Bari records different descriptions of this wrapping style; selecting one detailed explanation and making it part of the hadith would exceed the selected sentence. The English comparisons’ parenthetical explanations are not imported.
  • Ihtiba is rendered sitting with knees drawn up and wrapped in a garment, following the lexical description in Fath al-Bari. The explicit absence of covering over the private parts is retained here and is not silently copied into the shorter next report. No legal interpretation is appended.

AI-assisted translation of the complete selected Arabic, compared with two visually inspected Arabic prints and two individually matched English comparison families. Complete working English

Qutaybah ibn Sa‘id related to us. He said: Layth related to us, from Ibn Shihab, from ‘Ubayd Allah ibn ‘Abd Allah ibn ‘Utbah, from Abu Sa‘id al-Khudri, who said:

The Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, forbade wrapping oneself in the samma’ manner, and a man sitting with his knees drawn up, wrapped in a single garment, with none of it covering his private parts.

Comparison findings

Qabisah ibn ‘Uqbah related to us. He said: Sufyan related to us, from Abu al-Zinad, from al-A‘raj, from Abu Hurayrah, who said:

The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, forbade two types of sale, limas and nibadh, and wrapping oneself in the samma’ manner, and a man sitting with his knees drawn up, wrapped in a single garment.

  • The full unnamed nephew-of-Ibn-Shihab relationship remains without supplying his personal name. Humayd ibn Abd al-Rahman’s intervening attribution and Abu Hurayrah’s return to speaking remain distinct, unlike the compressed Khan-family account. Both printed witnesses retain those transitions.

Complete working English

Ishaq related to us. He said: Ya‘qub ibn Ibrahim related to us. He said: The nephew of Ibn Shihab related to us, from his uncle, who said: Humayd ibn ‘Abd al-Rahman ibn ‘Awf informed me that Abu Hurayrah said:

Abu Bakr sent me during that Hajj among those making proclamations on the Day of Sacrifice. We proclaimed at Mina: “Take notice: no polytheist is to perform Hajj after this year, and no one is to go around the House naked.”

Humayd ibn ‘Abd al-Rahman said: Then the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, sent ‘Ali after them and instructed him to proclaim Bara’ah.

Abu Hurayrah said: ‘Ali proclaimed with us among the people of Mina on the Day of Sacrifice: “No polytheist is to perform Hajj after this year, and no one is to go around the House naked.”

Complete working English

‘Abd al-‘Aziz ibn ‘Abd Allah related to us. He said: Ibn Abi al-Mawali related to me, from Muhammad ibn al-Munkadir, who said:

I went in to Jabir ibn ‘Abd Allah while he was praying wrapped in a garment, with his rida laid aside. When he finished, we said: “Abu ‘Abd Allah, do you pray with your rida laid aside?” He said: “Yes. I wanted ignorant people like you to see me. I saw the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, praying like this.”

The Quranic phrase within the report corresponds to 37:177; no surrounding verse or commentary is inserted.
  • Khamis is retained with the source’s own meaning the army. Safiyyah’s identity, the instruction to Dihyah and Thabit’s question to Abu Hamzah remain. نفسها is provisionally Herself, followed by the source’s own explanation of freeing and marrying; it is not silently replaced by an explanatory quotation. Sawiq (a preparation of ground roasted grain) and hays (a mixed food) remain source terms. The uncertainty about sawiq remains. No curd or other recipe ingredient from commentary is inserted.

AI-assisted translation of the complete selected Arabic, compared with two visually inspected Arabic prints and two individually matched English comparison families. Omissions, shared references and wording variants remain documented. Independent Arabic, English, lexical, chapter and reference review is pending; this is not certification.

Complete working English

Ya‘qub ibn Ibrahim related to us. He said: Isma‘il ibn ‘Ulayyah related to us. He said: ‘Abd al-‘Aziz ibn Suhayb related to us, from Anas:

That the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, attacked Khaybar. We prayed the dawn prayer near it in the darkness before daybreak. The Prophet of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, mounted, and Abu Talhah mounted, with me riding behind Abu Talhah. The Prophet of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, rode swiftly through an alley of Khaybar, and my knee was touching the thigh of the Prophet of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace. Then he drew the izar away from his thigh, until I could see the whiteness of the thigh of the Prophet of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace. When he entered the town, he said: “Allah is greater! Khaybar is ruined! When we descend into a people’s courtyard, then evil is the morning of those who were warned.” He said it three times.

He said: The people came out to their work and said: “Muhammad!” ‘Abd al-‘Aziz said: And some of our companions said: “And the khamis”—meaning the army.

He said: We took it by force. The captives were gathered, and Dihyah came and said: “Prophet of Allah, give me a female captive from among the captives.” He said: “Go and take a female captive.” He took Safiyyah bint Huyayy. A man came to the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, and said: “Prophet of Allah, you have given Dihyah Safiyyah bint Huyayy, the leading lady of Qurayzah and al-Nadir. She is fit only for you.” He said: “Call him with her.” He brought her, and when the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, looked at her, he said: “Take a female captive from among the captives other than her.” He said: The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, freed her and married her. Thabit said to him: “Abu Hamzah, what did he give her as her marriage payment?” He said: “Herself. He freed her and married her.” When he was on the journey, Umm Sulaym prepared her for him and brought her to him during the night. In the morning the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, was a bridegroom. He said: “Whoever has anything, let him bring it.” He spread out a leather sheet. One man would bring dates, and another would bring ghee. He said: I think he also mentioned sawiq. He said: They mixed a hays, and that was the wedding feast of the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace.

Unchanged Arabic report

[364] حدثنا يعقوب بن إبراهيم قال: حدثنا إسماعيل بن علية قال: حدثنا عبد العزيز بن صهيب عن أنس أن رسول الله ﷺ غزا خيبر فصلينا عندها صلاة الغداة بغلس فركب نبي الله ﷺ وركب أبو طلحة وأنا رديف أبي طلحة فأجرى نبي الله ﷺ في زقاق خيبر وإن ركبتي لتمس فخذ نبي الله ﷺ ثم حسر الإزار عن فخذه حتى إني أنظر إلى بياض فخذ نبي الله ﷺ فلما دخل القرية قال الله أكبر خربت خيبر إنا إذا نزلنا بساحة قوم { فساء صباح المنذرين } قالها ثلاثا قال وخرج القوم إلى أعمالهم فقالوا محمد قال عبد العزيز، وقال بعض أصحابنا والخميس يعني الجيش قال: فأصبناها عنوة فجمع السبي فجاء دحية، فقال: يا نبي الله أعطني جارية من السبي قال اذهب فخذ جارية فأخذ صفية بنت حيي فجاء رجل إلى النبي ﷺ، فقال: يا نبي الله أعطيت دحية صفية بنت حيي سيدة قريظة والنضير لا تصلح إلا لك قال ادعوه بها فجاء بها فلما نظر إليها النبي ﷺ قال: خذ جارية من السبي غيرها قال: فأعتقها النبي ﷺ وتزوجها، فقال له ثابت يا أبا حمزة ما أصدقها قال نفسها أعتقها وتزوجها حتى إذا كان بالطريق جهزتها له أم سليم فأهدتها له من الليل فأصبح النبي ﷺ عروسا، فقال من كان عنده شيء فليجئ به وبسط نطعا فجعل الرجل يجيء بالتمر وجعل الرجل يجيء بالسمن قال وأحسبه قد ذكر السويق قال: فحاسوا حيسا فكانت وليمة رسول الله ﷺ

Complete working English

Abu al-Yaman related to us. He said: Shu‘ayb informed us, from al-Zuhri, who said: ‘Urwah informed me that ‘A’ishah said:

The Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, used to pray Fajr, and some believing women would attend with him, wrapped in their cloaks. Then they would return to their homes, and no one would recognize them.

Comparison findings

  • This is one selected Arabic numbered entry with a later Hisham ibn Urwah / his father / Aishah transmission. Bewley includes both statements under 366; the pinned Khan-family EPUB numbers them separately as 1.8.369 and 1.8.370. Both comparison bodies were inspected and their exact segment coverage is recorded; neither part is dropped or turned into an extra reader entry.
  • Khamisah is a marked or patterned wrap; anbijaniyyah is an unmarked heavy woollen wrap. Source terms remain instead of importing the English comparison’s long shirt. The single glance, just now and present fear remain. The historical print has حدثني at Ibn Shihab where the selected and modern print have حدثنا; the selected chain is unchanged. Fath al-Bari discusses the wording and garment terms; its interpretation is not added to the report.

Complete working English

Ahmad ibn Yunus related to us. He said: Ibrahim ibn Sa‘d related to us. He said: Ibn Shihab related to us, from ‘Urwah, from ‘A’ishah:

That the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, prayed in a khamisah with markings. He glanced once at its markings. When he finished, he said: “Take this khamisah of mine to Abu Jahm and bring me Abu Jahm’s anbijaniyyah, for it distracted me just now from my prayer.”

And Hisham ibn ‘Urwah said, from his father, from ‘A’ishah: The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“I was looking at its marking while I was praying, and I fear that it may distract me.”

Complete working English

‘Abd Allah ibn Yusuf related to us. He said: Al-Layth related to us, from Yazid, from Abu al-Khayr, from ‘Uqbah ibn ‘Amir, who said:

A silk farruj was given to the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace. He put it on and prayed in it. Then, when he finished, he pulled it off forcefully, as though disliking it, and said: “This is not fitting for the God-fearing.”

Hadith 369

AI-assisted working translation. Independent review pending.

Open this hadith →

Comparison findings

  • The complete chain is Awn ibn Abi Juhayfah from his father. The Bewley introductory line shortens this differently; it does not replace the selected chain. The two rakahs, absent from that comparison’s body, remain.
  • Ablution water retains the source’s wording without adding used or left over. The garment being tucked up is retained without adding visible feet or sleeves. The people and animals pass in front of the spear; no wording is added that puts them between the worshipper and the spear. Both printed witnesses retain the full sequence.

AI-assisted translation of the complete selected Arabic, compared with two visually inspected Arabic prints and two individually matched English comparison families. Omissions, shared references and wording variants remain documented. Independent Arabic, English, lexical, chapter and reference review is pending; this is not certification.

Complete working English

Muhammad ibn ‘Ar‘arah related to us. He said: ‘Umar ibn Abi Za’idah related to me, from ‘Awn ibn Abi Juhayfah, from his father, who said:

I saw the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, in a red leather tent. I saw Bilal take the ablution water of the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, and I saw the people rushing for that water. Whoever obtained some wiped himself with it, and whoever did not obtain any took some of the moisture from his companion’s hand. Then I saw Bilal take a short spear and plant it. The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, came out in a red outfit, with his garment tucked up, and led the people in two rak‘ahs facing the spear. I saw people and animals passing in front of the spear.

Hadith 370

AI-assisted working translation. Independent review pending.

Open this hadith →

Comparison findings

  • The pulpit is of tamarisk from al-Ghabah. The unknown maker and woman remain unnamed. Mawla is retained rather than choosing an unexpressed legal relationship. Stepping backwards is explicit. The historical print and Khan-family comparison include a second recitation; the selected and modern print do not, so none is inserted.
  • The closing Abu Abd Allah / Ali ibn Abd Allah / Ahmad ibn Hanbal discussion is a visible Transmission note, not Prophetic speech. The source’s mercy honorific and final question to Ahmad about hearing from Sufyan, with his answer No, remain. The matched Khan-family body abridges this discussion; Bewley also omits the compiler attribution and mercy honorific. The selected pronoun He said is not silently replaced with a stronger identification of the speaker. Fath al-Bari discusses the exchange separately.

AI-assisted translation of the complete selected Arabic, compared with two visually inspected Arabic prints and two individually matched English comparison families. Omissions, shared references and wording variants remain documented. Independent Arabic, English, lexical, chapter and reference review is pending; this is not certification.

Complete working English

‘Ali ibn ‘Abd Allah related to us. He said: Sufyan related to us. He said: Abu Hazim related to us. He said:

They asked Sahl ibn Sa‘d what the pulpit was made of. He said: “There is no one left among the people who knows more about it than I do. It is made of tamarisk from al-Ghabah. So-and-so, the mawla of such-and-such a woman, made it for the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace. The Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, stood on it when it had been made and set in place. He faced the qiblah and said ‘Allah is greater,’ and the people stood behind him. He recited and bowed, and the people bowed behind him. Then he raised his head, stepped backwards and prostrated on the ground. Then he returned to the pulpit, bowed and raised his head. Then he stepped backwards until he prostrated on the ground. That was what he did.”

Abu ‘Abd Allah said: ‘Ali ibn ‘Abd Allah said: Ahmad ibn Hanbal, may Allah have mercy on him, asked me about this hadith. He said: “I only meant that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, was higher than the people. So, according to this hadith, there is no harm in the imam being higher than the people.” He said: I said: “Sufyan ibn ‘Uyaynah used to be asked about this often. Did you not hear it from him?” He said: “No.”

Hadith 371

AI-assisted working translation. Independent review pending.

Open this hadith →

Comparison findings

  • The uncertainty lower leg or shoulder remains. The selected and modern print say tree trunks; the historical print and English comparisons specify date-palm trunks. No tree species is inserted. The upper room, month-long oath, visit, seated imam with standing companions, every instruction and twenty-nine-day reply remain.
  • This selected report ends its posture instructions with praying standing. The instruction to pray seated that appears in other versions is not imported. No additional legal ruling about the oath or posture is appended.

AI-assisted translation of the complete selected Arabic, compared with two visually inspected Arabic prints and two individually matched English comparison families. Omissions, shared references and wording variants remain documented. Independent Arabic, English, lexical, chapter and reference review is pending; this is not certification.

Complete working English

Muhammad ibn ‘Abd al-Rahim related to us. He said: Yazid ibn Harun related to us. He said: Humayd al-Tawil informed us, from Anas ibn Malik:

That the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, fell from his horse and his lower leg or shoulder was grazed. He swore to stay away from his wives for a month and stayed in an upper room of his whose steps were made of tree trunks. His companions came to visit him. He led them in prayer while sitting, and they were standing. When he gave the salutation, he said: “The imam is appointed only to be followed. When he says ‘Allah is greater,’ say ‘Allah is greater’; when he bows, bow; when he prostrates, prostrate; and if he prays standing, pray standing.” He came down after twenty-nine days. They said: “Messenger of Allah, you swore for a month.” He said: “The month is twenty-nine days.”

Complete working English

Musaddad related to us, from Khalid, who said: Sulayman al-Shaybani related to us, from ‘Abd Allah ibn Shaddad, from Maymunah, who said:

The Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, used to pray with me beside him while I was menstruating. Sometimes his garment touched me when he prostrated. She said: He used to pray on a khumrah.

Hadith 373

AI-assisted working translation. Independent review pending.

Open this hadith →

Comparison findings

  • The source’s initial Abd Allah remains unexpanded; the historical print adds ibn Yusuf. The pronoun his grandmother is retained because Fath al-Bari records different identifications of its referent, Anas or Ishaq. No determination is silently inserted. The historical print adds an honorific after Mulaykah which is absent in the selected and modern text; none is added.
  • The source says our mat and sprinkled it, not my mat and washed it as in the Khan-family comparison. The orphan and old woman remain unnamed, and no ablution or reason for sprinkling is imported from commentary. The complete invitation, meal, arrangement, two rakahs and departure remain.

AI-assisted translation of the complete selected Arabic, compared with two visually inspected Arabic prints and two individually matched English comparison families. Omissions, shared references and wording variants remain documented. Independent Arabic, English, lexical, chapter and reference review is pending; this is not certification.

Complete working English

‘Abd Allah related to us. He said: Malik informed us, from Ishaq ibn ‘Abd Allah ibn Abi Talhah, from Anas ibn Malik:

That his grandmother Mulaykah invited the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, to some food she had prepared for him. He ate some of it, then said: “Get up, so that I may pray for you.” Anas said: I went to a mat of ours that had become black from long use and sprinkled it with water. The Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, stood, and the orphan and I stood in a row behind him, with the old woman behind us. The Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, led us in two rak‘ahs, then left.

Complete working English

Isma‘il related to us. He said: Malik related to me, from Abu al-Nadr, the mawla of ‘Umar ibn ‘Ubayd Allah, from Abu Salamah ibn ‘Abd al-Rahman, from ‘A’ishah, the wife of the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, who said:

I used to sleep in front of the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, with my legs in the direction of his qiblah. When he prostrated, he nudged me and I drew my legs in. When he stood, I stretched them out. She said: The houses in those days had no lamps.

Open this hadith →

Comparison findings

  • Both Arabic prints contain this report, including the uncertainty I think he said and the statement attributed to Hudhayfah. The historical print uses related to us at the initial Al-Salt and Mahdi links where the selected and modern text use informed us. The selected form remains.
  • The pinned English EPUB proceeds directly from 1.8.385l (al-Mughirah’s ablution report) to 1.8.385u (Ibn Buhaynah’s arm-position report). This Hudhayfah account is not present between them. Both exact records and their source positions are retained as absence evidence. Bewley 382 supplies a separate English comparison. Fath al-Bari discusses different recension placement of these two chapters; that discussion does not supply missing English text or authorize changing the selected chapter order.
  • Sunnah.com modern Bukhari 389 independently displays the Arabic account and an English body retaining the uncertainty. Its deprecated 1.8.385 label is not assumed to identify an item in this EPUB; the literal surrounding source labels remain 385l and 385u.

Omissions, shared references and wording variants remain documented. Independent Arabic, English, lexical, chapter and reference review is pending; this is not certification.

Complete working English

Al-Salt ibn Muhammad informed us; Mahdi informed us, from Wasil, from Abu Wa’il, from Hudhayfah:

He saw a man who did not complete his bowing or prostration. When he finished his prayer, Hudhayfah said to him: “You have not prayed.”

He said: I think he said: “If you died, you would die following other than the way of Muhammad, may Allah bless him and grant him peace.”

Complete working English

Yahya ibn Bukayr informed us; Bakr ibn Mudar related to us, from Ja‘far, from Ibn Hurmuz, from ‘Abd Allah ibn Malik ibn Buhaynah:

That when the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, prayed, he spread his arms apart until the whiteness of his armpits could be seen.

And al-Layth said: Ja‘far ibn Rabi‘ah related to me something similar.

Complete working English

‘Amr ibn ‘Abbas related to us. He said: Ibn al-Mahdi related to us. He said: Mansur ibn Sa‘d related to us, from Maymun ibn Siyah, from Anas ibn Malik, who said: The Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“Whoever prays our prayer, faces our qiblah and eats what we slaughter is the Muslim who has the protection of Allah and the protection of His Messenger. So do not betray Allah with regard to His protection.”

Hadith 385

AI-assisted working translation. Independent review pending.

Open this hadith →

Comparison findings

  • The selected entry contains a Prophetic statement, an Ibn Abi Maryam / Yahya / Humayd / Anas transmission, and a separate Ali / Khalid / Humayd account of Maymun questioning Anas. All remain in source order. The modern print divides this material across 392 and 393, while the selected source and Bewley retain it under one reference. The matched Khan-family 1.8.387 gives the first and last bodies but omits the middle transmission.
  • The modern print uses نحروا where the selected and historical print use ذبحوا; the selected Arabic remains unchanged. The wording slaughter as we slaughter in the first statement and eats what we slaughter in the last answer remains distinct. The exception بحقها is rendered by their due right without importing a list of legal grounds. The declaration, complete conditions, property and blood, reckoning, and final rights and obligations all remain. The historical print expands Yahya to Yahya ibn Ayyub and adds an honorific after Allah; neither is inserted into the selected report.

AI-assisted translation of the complete selected Arabic, compared with two visually inspected Arabic prints and two individually matched English comparison families. Omissions, shared references and wording variants remain documented. Independent Arabic, English, lexical, chapter and reference review is pending; this is not certification.

Complete working English

Nu‘aym related to us. He said: Ibn al-Mubarak related to us, from Humayd al-Tawil, from Anas ibn Malik, who said: The Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“I have been commanded to fight the people until they say: ‘There is no god but Allah.’ When they say it, pray our prayer, face our qiblah and slaughter as we slaughter, their blood and property become forbidden to us except by their due right, and their reckoning is with Allah.”

Ibn Abi Maryam said: Yahya informed us; Humayd related to us; Anas related to us, from the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace.

And ‘Ali ibn ‘Abd Allah said: Khalid ibn al-Harith related to us. He said: Humayd related to us. He said:

Maymun ibn Siyah asked Anas ibn Malik. He said: “Abu Hamzah, what makes a person’s blood and property inviolable?” He said: “Whoever testifies that there is no god but Allah, faces our qiblah, prays our prayer and eats what we slaughter is a Muslim. He has what a Muslim has, and owes what a Muslim owes.”

Complete working English

‘Ali ibn ‘Abd Allah related to us. He said: Sufyan related to us. He said: Al-Zuhri related to us, from ‘Ata’ ibn Yazid, from Abu Ayyub al-Ansari, that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“When you go to relieve yourselves, do not face the qiblah or turn your backs to it, but face east or west.” Abu Ayyub said: We came to al-Sham and found lavatories built facing the qiblah, so we would turn aside and seek the forgiveness of Allah Most High.

And from al-Zuhri, from ‘Ata’, who said: I heard Abu Ayyub, from the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, relate the same.

Complete working English

Al-Humaydi related to us. He said: Sufyan related to us. He said: ‘Amr ibn Dinar related to us. He said:

We asked Ibn ‘Umar about a man who had gone around the House for ‘umrah but had not gone between al-Safa and al-Marwah: could he have intercourse with his wife? He said: The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, came, went around the House seven times, prayed two rak‘ahs behind the Station, and went between al-Safa and al-Marwah. “You have had an excellent example in the Messenger of Allah.” We asked Jabir ibn ‘Abd Allah, and he said: “He must not approach her until he has gone between al-Safa and al-Marwah.”

  • The selected Sayf remains unexpanded although the modern print adds a name explanation. The full narrator chain, arrival after the Prophet had left, Bilal between the two doors, the two pillars and both sets of two rakahs remain. The selected relative-direction wording is rendered on the left as you enter; no unmentioned architectural reconstruction is added.
  • This report and the next give different accounts of prayer inside the Kaaba. Both are preserved without reconciling one into the other. Fath al-Bari discusses the distinct accounts and the phrase facing the Kaaba; that discussion remains separate.

AI-assisted translation of the complete selected Arabic, compared with two visually inspected Arabic prints and two individually matched English comparison families. Omissions, shared references and wording variants remain documented. Independent Arabic, English, lexical, chapter and reference review is pending; this is not certification.

Complete working English

Musaddad related to us. He said: Yahya related to us, from Sayf, who said: I heard Mujahid say:

Someone came to Ibn ‘Umar and said to him: “Here is the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace; he has entered the Ka‘bah.” Ibn ‘Umar said: I came, but the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, had already come out. I found Bilal standing between the two doors. I asked Bilal: “Did the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, pray inside the Ka‘bah?” He said: “Yes, two rak‘ahs between the two pillars on the left as you enter. Then he came out and prayed two rak‘ahs facing the Ka‘bah.”

Hadith 390

Open this hadith →

Comparison findings

  • The dual honorific after al-Bara ibn Azib, sixteen-or-seventeen uncertainty and unnamed man remain. The historical print shortens the companion name and omits the honorific; the selected and modern wording remains. The narrator’s who were the Jews phrase is retained as source wording, without any modern generalization.
  • The selected Quran quotations correspond to portions of 2:144 and 2:142 and remain in their supplied extent. The third-person testimony is retained rather than silently converted to I testify; Fath al-Bari discusses that wording. Bayt al-Maqdis remains the source place name. No companion name, exact date or additional historical explanation is inserted.

AI-assisted translation of the complete selected Arabic, compared with two visually inspected Arabic prints and two individually matched English comparison families. Omissions, shared references and wording variants remain documented. Independent Arabic, English, lexical, chapter and reference review is pending; this is not certification.

Complete working English

‘Abd Allah ibn Raja’ related to us. He said: Isra’il related to us, from Abu Ishaq, from al-Bara’ ibn ‘Azib, may Allah Most High be pleased with them both, who said:

The Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, had prayed towards Bayt al-Maqdis for sixteen or seventeen months. The Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, wished to be directed towards the Ka‘bah. Then Allah revealed: “We have certainly seen the turning of your face towards the sky.” He turned towards the Ka‘bah. The foolish among the people—who were the Jews—said: “What has turned them from the qiblah they had been facing? Say: To Allah belong the east and the west. He guides whom He wills to a straight path.” A man prayed with the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, then went out after praying. He passed some of the Ansar in the ‘Asr prayer, facing Bayt al-Maqdis. He said that he testified that he had prayed with the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, and that he had faced towards the Ka‘bah. The people turned until they faced towards the Ka‘bah.

Complete working English

The Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, used to pray on his mount wherever it turned. When he intended the obligatory prayer, he dismounted and faced the qiblah.

Comparison findings

  • Ibrahim’s uncertainty is a separate transmission note between the opening action and the continuation. It is not attributed to the Prophet, replaced by a known prayer count from a different account, or silently omitted. The full Uthman / Jarir / Mansur / Ibrahim / Alqamah / Abd Allah chain remains; Abd Allah is not expanded to Ibn Masud from the historical print.
  • The selected dual legs, two prostrations, salutation, turned face, statement about forgetting and final ordered instructions remain. No direction-of-head-turning explanation or extra spoken salutation from the English comparison is inserted. Seek what is correct provisionally renders تحري الصواب without choosing a juristic rule for resolving uncertainty.

AI-assisted translation of the complete selected Arabic, compared with two visually inspected Arabic prints and two individually matched English comparison families. Omissions, shared references and wording variants remain documented. Independent Arabic, English, lexical, chapter and reference review is pending; this is not certification.

Complete working English

‘Uthman related to us. He said: Jarir related to us, from Mansur, from Ibrahim, from ‘Alqamah, who said: ‘Abd Allah said:

The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, prayed.

Ibrahim said: I do not know whether he added or omitted.

When he gave the salutation, he was asked: “Messenger of Allah, has something new happened in the prayer?” He said: “What is that?” They said: “You prayed such-and-such.” He bent his legs, faced the qiblah and performed two prostrations, then gave the salutation. When he turned his face towards us, he said: “If something new had happened in the prayer, I would have informed you of it. But I am only a human being like you; I forget as you forget. So when I forget, remind me. If any of you is uncertain in his prayer, let him seek what is correct and complete it on that basis, then give the salutation, then perform two prostrations.”

Comparison findings

  • The selected opening says I agreed with my Lord; the English comparisons reverse the grammatical subject. The working translation retains the selected first-person subject. The three matters, both requests, righteous and wicked speakers, jealousy, and supplied Quran quotations remain complete. Complete working English

‘Amr ibn ‘Awn related to us. He said: Hushaym related to us, from Humayd, from Anas, who said: ‘Umar said:

I agreed with my Lord regarding three matters. I said: “Messenger of Allah, if only we took the Station of Ibrahim as a place of prayer.” Then “Take the Station of Ibrahim as a place of prayer” was revealed. And the verse of the veil: I said: “Messenger of Allah, if only you ordered your wives to veil themselves, for both the righteous and the wicked speak to them.” Then the verse of the veil was revealed. The wives of the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, joined together in jealousy concerning him. I said to them: “Perhaps his Lord, if he divorces you, will give him wives better than you in your place.” Then this verse was revealed.

Complete working English

‘Abd Allah ibn Yusuf related to us. He said: Malik ibn Anas informed us, from ‘Abd Allah ibn Dinar, from ‘Abd Allah ibn ‘Umar, who said:

While the people were at Quba’ in the morning prayer, someone came to them and said: “Qur’an has been revealed to the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, tonight, and he has been commanded to face the Ka‘bah, so face it.” Their faces had been towards al-Sham, so they turned towards the Ka‘bah.

Comparison findings

  • The selected dual under his feet remains, although both inspected prints use singular under his foot. The uncertainty or his Lord is between him and the qiblah remains, without adding a theological interpretation or resolving it into the preceding clause. AI-assisted translation of the complete selected Arabic, compared with two visually inspected Arabic prints and two individually matched English comparison families. Omissions, shared references and wording variants remain documented. Independent Arabic, English, lexical, chapter and reference review is pending; this is not certification.

Complete working English

Qutaybah related to us. He said: Isma‘il ibn Ja‘far related to us, from Humayd, from Anas:

The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, saw phlegm in the direction of the qiblah. It distressed him so much that it could be seen in his face. He stood and scraped it off with his hand. He said: “When one of you stands in his prayer, he is conversing privately with his Lord—or his Lord is between him and the qiblah. So none of you should spit towards his qiblah, but to his left or under his feet.” Then he took the edge of his cloak, spat into it, and folded part of it over the other. He said: “Or he should do this.”

Hadith 401

AI-assisted working translation. Independent review pending.

Comparison findings

  • The digital word يمينة has a final ta marbutah where both inspected prints read يمينه. A declared one-letter correction follows the printed possessive form; the unchanged original, before/after span and both readings remain accessible.
  • This remains a distinct report with its own chain and two Messenger of Allah honorifics. Bewley 401 abbreviates its body with a pointer to 400; the complete selected report and individually matched EPUB 1.8.403 remain. The different reporting verbs related to him in 400 and informed him here are preserved.

AI-assisted translation of the complete selected Arabic, compared with two visually inspected Arabic prints and two individually matched English comparison families. Omissions, shared references and wording variants remain documented. Independent Arabic, English, lexical, chapter and reference review is pending; this is not certification.

Complete working English

Yahya ibn Bukayr related to us. He said: Al-Layth related to us, from ‘Uqayl, from Ibn Shihab, from Humayd ibn ‘Abd al-Rahman, that Abu Hurayrah and Abu Sa‘id informed him:

The Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, saw phlegm on the wall of the mosque. The Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, took a pebble and scraped it off. Then he said: “When one of you spits phlegm, he should not spit it in front of his face or to his right. He should spit to his left or under his left foot.”

Arabic used in the reader · declared correction

[401] حدثنا يحيى بن بكير قال: حدثنا الليث عن عقيل عن ابن شهاب عن حميد بن عبد الرحمن أن أبا هريرة وأبا سعيد أخبراه أن رسول الله ﷺ رأى نخامة في حائط المسجد فتناول رسول الله ﷺ حصاة فحتها، ثم قال إذا تنخم أحدكم فلا يتنخم قبل وجهه ولا عن يمينه وليبصق عن يساره أو تحت قدمه اليسرى

Recorded Arabic correction

Applied only to this personal prototype. AI-evaluated; independent review pending.

Both inspected Arabic prints read يمينه, with the possessive ha, where the digital paragraph has يمينة. Only this final letter is corrected; every other original code point remains unchanged.

Original wording:

ولا عن يمينة

Reader wording:

ولا عن يمينه

Hadith 404

AI-assisted working translation. Independent review pending.

Open this hadith →

Comparison findings

  • The narrator remains Abu Said alone; Abu Hurayrah is not inserted from the nearby dual transmissions. The closing al-Zuhri hearing from Humayd is preserved as another chain, including something similar, although the EPUB omits it and Bewley shortens it. Fath al-Bari identifies this closing phrase as al-Zuhri’s explicit hearing from Humayd. The pebble is singular, unlike a plural English rendering.

AI-assisted translation of the complete selected Arabic, compared with two visually inspected Arabic prints and two individually matched English comparison families. Omissions, shared references and wording variants remain documented. Independent Arabic, English, lexical, chapter and reference review is pending; this is not certification.

Complete working English

‘Ali related to us. He said: Sufyan related to us; al-Zuhri related to us, from Humayd ibn ‘Abd al-Rahman, from Abu Sa‘id:

The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, saw phlegm in the qiblah of the mosque and scraped it off with a pebble. Then he forbade a man to spit in front of him or to his right, but to his left or under his left foot.

And from al-Zuhri: he heard Humayd relate from Abu Sa‘id something similar.

Complete working English

Adam related to us. He said: Shu‘bah related to us. He said: Qatadah related to us. He said: I heard Anas ibn Malik say: The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“Spitting in the mosque is a sin, and its expiation is to bury it.”

AI-assisted working translation. Independent review pending.

Comparison findings

  • The full chain retains Hammam without importing Ibn Munabbih, and the selected related to us for Abd al-Razzaq; the historical print uses informed us and adds Most High after Allah. Neither difference changes the selected text.
  • The source’s as long as he is in his place of prayer, angel to his right, unqualified foot and bury it remain. No recording angel or left-foot detail from a comparison is supplied. Commentary about the mosque floor and burial remains outside the hadith wording.

AI-assisted translation of the complete selected Arabic, compared with two visually inspected Arabic prints and two individually matched English comparison families. Complete working English

Ishaq ibn Nasr related to us. He said: ‘Abd al-Razzaq related to us, from Ma‘mar, from Hammam, who heard Abu Hurayrah, from the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, who said:

“When one of you stands for prayer, he should not spit in front of him, for he is conversing privately with Allah as long as he is in his place of prayer; nor to his right, for there is an angel to his right. He should spit to his left or under his foot and bury it.”

Hadith 407

AI-assisted working translation. Independent review pending.

Open this hadith →

Comparison findings

  • Both uncertainty markers remain: the alternate description of visible dislike and the or connecting the statements about prayer. The Khan-family body flattens the first alternative; the working translation follows the selected Arabic and the prints. The complete cloak demonstration and closing alternative remain. The chapter title’s sudden need to spit is not inserted into the report itself.

AI-assisted translation of the complete selected Arabic, compared with two visually inspected Arabic prints and two individually matched English comparison families. Omissions, shared references and wording variants remain documented. Independent Arabic, English, lexical, chapter and reference review is pending; this is not certification.

Complete working English

Malik ibn Isma‘il related to us. He said: Zuhayr related to us. He said: Humayd related to us, from Anas:

The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, saw phlegm in the direction of the qiblah and scraped it off with his hand. His dislike was seen—or his dislike of it and how distressing it was to him were seen. He said: “When one of you stands in his prayer, he is conversing privately with his Lord—or his Lord is between him and his qiblah. So he should not spit towards his qiblah, but to his left or under his foot.” Then he took the edge of his cloak, spat into it and folded part of it over the other. He said: “Or he should do this.”

Unchanged Arabic report

[407] حدثنا مالك بن إسماعيل قال: حدثنا زهير قال: حدثنا حميد عن أنس أن النبي ﷺ رأى نخامة في القبلة فحكها بيده ورؤي منه كراهية أو رؤي كراهيته لذلك وشدته عليه، وقال إن أحدكم إذا قام في صلاته فإنما يناجي ربه أو ربه بينه وبين قبلته فلا يبزقن في قبلته ولكن عن يساره أو تحت قدمه ثم أخذ طرف ردائه فبزق فيه ورد بعضه على بعض قال أو يفعل هكذا

Complete working English

‘Abd Allah ibn Yusuf related to us. He said: Malik informed us, from Nafi‘, from ‘Abd Allah ibn ‘Umar:

The Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, held a race between horses that had been trained, starting from al-Hafya’, with Thaniyyat al-Wada‘ as its finish. He held a race between horses that had not been trained, from al-Thaniyyah to the mosque of Banu Zurayq. ‘Abd Allah ibn ‘Umar was among those who raced them.

Hadith 411

AI-assisted working translation. Independent review pending.

Open this hadith →

Comparison findings

  • The selected introduction begins And Ibrahim said. It is not changed to a direct related-to-us transmission, nor is Ibn Tahman inserted from the historical print. The original Anas honorific remains. Both complete request-and-refusal cycles, both reductions of the load, our sight and the final dirham remain. The historical print is shorter in the repeated exchange, while the modern print agrees with the selected full sequence.
  • Eagerness provisionally renders حرص without adding a moral explanation. No amount, tax category, battle date or ransom circumstances from commentary are inserted. Both prints preserve a separate compiler definition of the chapter’s date-bunch term, absent from the digital chapter. It is supplied separately as Arabic chapter material with the dual/plural vowel distinction retained; English is pending.

AI-assisted translation of the complete selected Arabic, compared with two visually inspected Arabic prints and two individually matched English comparison families. Omissions, shared references and wording variants remain documented. Independent Arabic, English, lexical, chapter and reference review is pending; this is not certification.

Complete working English

And Ibrahim said, from ‘Abd al-‘Aziz ibn Suhayb, from Anas, may Allah Most High be pleased with him, who said:

The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, was brought wealth from Bahrayn. He said: “Spread it out in the mosque.” It was the greatest amount of wealth that had been brought to the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace.

The Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, went out to the prayer without looking towards it. When he had finished the prayer, he came and sat beside it. There was no one he saw without giving him something.

Then al-‘Abbas came to him and said: “Messenger of Allah, give me some, for I paid the ransom for myself and paid the ransom for ‘Aqil.” The Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said to him: “Take.” He scooped some into his garment, then tried to lift it but could not. He said: “Messenger of Allah, tell one of them to lift it up to me.” He said: “No.” He said: “Then lift it onto me yourself.” He said: “No.” So he poured out some of it, then tried to lift it. He said: “Messenger of Allah, tell one of them to lift it onto me.” He said: “No.” He said: “Then lift it onto me yourself.” He said: “No.” So he poured out some of it, then lifted it, placed it on his shoulders and went away.

The Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, kept following him with his gaze until he disappeared from our sight, astonished at his eagerness. The Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, did not get up while a dirham of it remained there.

Complete working English

‘Abd Allah ibn Yusuf related to us; Malik informed us, from Ishaq ibn ‘Abd Allah, who heard Anas say:

I found the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, in the mosque with some people. I stood there. He said to me: “Did Abu Talhah send you?” I said: “Yes.” He said: “For food?” I said: “Yes.” He said to those with him: “Get up.” He set off, and I went ahead of them.

Comparison findings

  • The visit, question including for you, indicated place, takbir, rows and two rakahs remain. The first third-person attribution and subsequent first-person account are retained, rather than harmonized into the comparison’s first-person opening. The fuller account at 415 is not copied here.

Omissions, shared references and wording variants remain documented. Independent Arabic, English, lexical, chapter and reference review is pending; this is not certification.

Complete working English

‘Abd Allah ibn Maslamah related to us. He said: Ibrahim ibn Sa‘d related to us, from Ibn Shihab, from Mahmud ibn al-Rabi‘, from ‘Itban ibn Malik:

The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, came to him at his home and said: “Where in your house would you like me to pray for you?” He said: I pointed out a place to him. The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said “Allah is greater,” and we formed rows behind him. He prayed two rak‘ahs.

Hadith 415

AI-assisted working translation. Independent review pending.

Open this hadith →

Comparison findings

  • Both inspected prints read سال الوادي, the valley flowed, where the selected digital text has سأل الوادي. A declared hamzah correction follows the prints; the complete original digital paragraph remains accessible, and no other source character changes.
  • The complete introductory attribution, deteriorating eyesight, rain, request, if Allah wills, morning arrival, permission, location, prayer and meal remain. The selected until he had entered (حتى دخل) is retained; the historical print and English comparisons use when he entered, a documented variant. The food name khazirah is retained without a recipe. Neighbourhood renders أهل الدار following the lexical explanation, not a narrower family-only group.
  • Both alternatives in Malik’s name remain. The speaker who makes the accusation and replies Allah and His Messenger know best is not newly named. Face-of-Allah wording is retained in both statements, without replacing it with a theological gloss. The closing Ibn Shihab/al-Husayn confirmation remains complete as a visible transmission note, although the Khan-family EPUB omits it. The historical print differs in the initial narrator name and uses after that in the closing note; neither change is imported.

AI-assisted translation of the complete selected Arabic, compared with two visually inspected Arabic prints and two individually matched English comparison families. Omissions, shared references and wording variants remain documented. Independent Arabic, English, lexical, chapter and reference review is pending; this is not certification.

Complete working English

Sa‘id ibn ‘Ufayr related to us. He said: Al-Layth related to me. He said: ‘Uqayl related to me, from Ibn Shihab, who said: Mahmud ibn al-Rabi‘ al-Ansari informed me:

‘Itban ibn Malik, who was one of the Companions of the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, and one of the Ansar who had witnessed Badr, came to the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, and said: “Messenger of Allah, my eyesight has deteriorated, and I lead my people in prayer. When the rains come, the valley between me and them flows, and I cannot reach their mosque to lead them in prayer. Messenger of Allah, I would like you to come and pray in my house so that I may take it as a place of prayer.” He said: The Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said: “I will do so, if Allah wills.”

‘Itban said: The Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, and Abu Bakr came in the morning when the day had advanced. The Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, asked permission to enter, and I gave him permission. He did not sit down until he had entered the house. Then he said: “Where in your house would you like me to pray?” He said: I pointed out a part of the house to him. The Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, stood and said “Allah is greater.” We stood and formed rows. He prayed two rak‘ahs, then gave the salutation.

He said: We kept him for some khazirah that we had prepared for him. He said: A number of men from the neighbourhood came into the house and gathered.

One of them said: “Where is Malik ibn al-Dukhayshin—or Ibn al-Dukhshun?” Some of them said: “That is a hypocrite who does not love Allah and His Messenger.” The Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said: “Do not say that. Do you not see that he has said ‘There is no god but Allah,’ desiring by that the face of Allah?” He said: “Allah and His Messenger know best.” He said: “We see his attention and his counsel directed towards the hypocrites.” The Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said: “Allah has forbidden the Fire to anyone who says ‘There is no god but Allah,’ seeking by that the face of Allah.”

Ibn Shihab said: Then I asked al-Husayn ibn Muhammad al-Ansari, who was one of Banu Salim and one of their leading men, about the account of Mahmud ibn al-Rabi‘, and he confirmed it.

Arabic used in the reader · declared correction

[415] حدثنا سعيد بن عفير قال: حدثني الليث قال: حدثني عقيل عن ابن شهاب قال: أخبرني محمود بن الربيع الأنصارى أن عتبان بن مالك وهو من أصحاب رسول الله ﷺ ممن شهد بدرا من الأنصار أنه أتى رسول الله ﷺ، فقال: يا رسول الله قد أنكرت بصري وأنا أصلي لقومي فإذا كانت الأمطار سال الوادي الذي بيني وبينهم لم أستطع أن آتي مسجدهم فأصلي بهم ووددت يا رسول الله أنك تأتيني فتصلي في بيتي فأتخذه مصلى قال، فقال رسول الله ﷺ: سأفعل إن شاء الله قال عتبان فغدا رسول الله ﷺ وأبو بكر حين ارتفع النهار فاستأذن رسول الله ﷺ فأذنت له فلم يجلس حتى دخل البيت، ثم قال أين تحب أن أصلي من بيتك قال: فأشرت له إلى ناحية من البيت فقام رسول الله ﷺ فكبر فقمنا فصففنا فصلى ركعتين ثم سلم قال وحبسناه على خزيرة صنعناها له قال: فثاب في البيت رجال من أهل الدار ذوو عدد فاجتمعوا، فقال قائل منهم أين مالك بن الدخيشن أو بن الدخشن، فقال بعضهم ذلك منافق لا يحب الله ورسوله، فقال رسول الله ﷺ: لا تقل ذلك ألا تراه قد قال لا إله إلا الله يريد بذلك وجه الله قال الله ورسوله أعلم قال: فإنا نرى وجهه ونصيحته إلى المنافقين قال رسول الله ﷺ: فإن الله قد حرم على النار من قال لا إله إلا الله يبتغي بذلك وجه الله قال ابن شهاب ثم سألت الحصين بن محمد الأنصارى وهو أحد بني سالم وهو من سراتهم عن حديث محمود بن الربيع فصدقه بذلك

Complete working English

Complete working English

Muhammad ibn al-Muthanna related to us. He said: Yahya related to us, from Hisham, who said: My father informed me, from ‘A’ishah:

Umm Habibah and Umm Salamah mentioned a church they had seen in Abyssinia in which there were pictures. They mentioned it to the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, and he said: “Those people, when a righteous man among them dies, build a place of worship over his grave and make those pictures in it. They are the worst of creation before Allah on the Day of Resurrection.”

No later purchase price, owners’ names or route date is supplied.
