Two sides provisionally renders عضادتيه; commentary also discusses doorpost/support senses. These remain lexical review items, without adding an architectural reconstruction. The selected opening O Allah, good of the Hereafter and forgive (not help) are preserved in the verse lines. The historical print adds عز وجل and another preposition before the Emigrants; neither addition replaces the selected text.

AI-assisted translation of the complete selected Arabic, compared with two visually inspected Arabic prints and two individually matched English comparison families. Omissions, shared references and wording variants remain documented. Independent Arabic, English, lexical, chapter and reference review is pending; this is not certification.

Complete working English

Musaddad related to us. He said: ‘Abd al-Warith related to us, from Abu al-Tayyah, from Anas, who said:

The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, arrived in Madinah and stayed in the upper part of Madinah among a clan called Banu ‘Amr ibn ‘Awf. The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, remained among them for fourteen nights. Then he sent for Banu al-Najjar, and they came wearing their swords. It is as though I am looking at the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, on his riding camel, with Abu Bakr behind him and a gathering of Banu al-Najjar around him, until he alighted in Abu Ayyub’s courtyard. He liked to pray wherever the prayer time overtook him, and he would pray in sheepfolds.

He ordered the mosque to be built and sent for a gathering of Banu al-Najjar. He said: “Banu al-Najjar, agree with me on a price for this walled garden of yours.” They said: “No, by Allah! We seek its price only from Allah.”

Anas said: It contained what I am telling you: graves of polytheists, ruins and date palms. The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, ordered the polytheists’ graves to be dug up, then the ruins to be levelled and the date palms to be cut down. They lined up the date palms at the qiblah of the mosque and made its two sides of stone. They began carrying the rocks while chanting verses. The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, was with them, saying:

“O Allah, there is no good except the good of the Hereafter,

so forgive the Ansar and the Emigrants.”

Chapter heading and introduction

Whether the graves of the polytheists of the time of ignorance may be dug up and their sites made into mosques, because of the Prophet’s saying, may Allah bless him and grant him peace: “Allah cursed the Jews; they took their prophets’ graves as mosques”

Complete working English

Sulayman ibn Harb related to us. He said: Shu‘bah related to us, from Abu al-Tayyah, from Anas, who said:

The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, used to pray in sheepfolds.

Then, later, I heard him say: “He used to pray in sheepfolds before the mosque was built.”

Complete working English

Muhammad related to us. He said: ‘Abdah informed us, from Hisham ibn ‘Urwah, from his father, from ‘A’ishah:

Umm Salamah mentioned to the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, a church she had seen in the land of Abyssinia, called Mariyah. She told him about the pictures she had seen in it. The Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said: “Those are people who, when a righteous servant—or a righteous man—among them dies, build a place of worship over his grave and make those pictures in it. They are the worst of creation before Allah.”

Complete working English

Abu al-Yaman related to us. He said: Shu‘ayb informed us, from al-Zuhri: ‘Ubayd Allah ibn ‘Abd Allah ibn ‘Utbah informed me that ‘A’ishah and ‘Abd Allah ibn ‘Abbas said:

When death came upon the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, he began putting a khamisah of his over his face. When it made him feel suffocated, he uncovered his face. While in that state, he said: “Allah’s curse be upon the Jews and the Christians. They took the graves of their prophets as places of prayer.” He was warning against what they had done.

Complete working English

Muhammad ibn Sinan related to us. He said: Hushaym related to us. He said: Sayyar—who is Abu al-Hakam—related to us. He said: Yazid al-Faqir related to us. He said: Jabir ibn ‘Abd Allah related to us. He said: The Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“I have been given five things that none of the prophets before me was given. I have been aided by fear over a distance of a month’s journey. The earth has been made for me a place of prayer and a means of purification, and whichever man of my community is overtaken by the time of prayer, let him pray. The spoils have been made lawful for me. A prophet used to be sent to his own people specifically, while I have been sent to all people. I have been given intercession.”

Hadith 428

AI-assisted working translation. Independent review pending.

Open this hadith →

Comparison findings

  • All four Arabic paragraphs remain, including both verse lines and the closing Aisha question and answer. The selected أعاجيب differs from تعاجيب in both prints; the selected lexical form is not silently corrected. The report keeps the put-down-or-fell and tent-or-hut uncertainties, every she-said attribution, the intervening third-person her private parts and the later return to first-person speech.
  • Wishah is retained as the garment/accessory term, with the selected description red and made of leather strips. No jewels, added punishment or explanatory name is imported from another comparison or parallel. The second verse line retains its pronoun He; English interpretation of the antecedent and the wishah term remains subject to independent review.

AI-assisted translation of the complete selected Arabic, compared with two visually inspected Arabic prints and two individually matched English comparison families. Omissions, shared references and wording variants remain documented. Independent Arabic, English, lexical, chapter and reference review is pending; this is not certification.

Complete working English

‘Ubayd ibn Isma‘il related to us. He said: Abu Usamah related to us, from Hisham, from his father, from ‘A’ishah:

A black slave woman belonged to an Arab clan. They set her free, and she stayed with them. She said: “A young girl of theirs went out wearing a red wishah made of leather strips.” She said: “She put it down, or it fell from her. A kite passed by it while it lay there, thought it was meat and snatched it.” She said: “They looked for it but did not find it.” She said: “They accused me of it.” She said: They began searching, until they even searched her private parts.

She said: “By Allah, I was standing with them when the kite passed by and dropped it.” She said: “It fell among them.” She said: “I said: ‘This is what you accused me of, as you claimed, while I am innocent of it. Here it is!’”

She said: She came to the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, and became a Muslim. ‘A’ishah said: She had a tent in the mosque, or a small hut. She said: She used to come to me and talk with me. She said: She never sat with me without saying:

“The day of the wishah is among the wonders of our Lord;

indeed, He rescued me from the town of unbelief.”

‘A’ishah said: I said to her: “Why is it that you never sit with me without saying this?” She said: She told me this account.

Unchanged Arabic report

Complete working English

Qutaybah ibn Sa‘id related to us. He said: ‘Abd al-‘Aziz ibn Abi Hazim related to us, from Abu Hazim, from Sahl ibn Sa‘d, who said:

The Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, came to Fatimah’s house and did not find ‘Ali in the house. He said: “Where is your cousin?” She said: “There was something between me and him. He became angry with me and went out, and did not take his midday rest with me.”

The Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said to someone: “Look where he is.” He came and said: “Messenger of Allah, he is lying asleep in the mosque.” The Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, came while he was lying there. His cloak had fallen from his side, and dust had settled on him. The Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, began wiping it off him, saying: “Get up, Abu Turab. Get up, Abu Turab.”

Complete working English

Yusuf ibn ‘Isa related to us. He said: Ibn Fudayl related to us, from his father, from Abu Hazim, from Abu Hurayrah, who said:

I saw seventy of the people of the Suffah, none of whom had a cloak. They had either a waist-wrapper or a covering that they had tied around their necks. Some reached halfway down the shins, and some reached the ankles. Each would gather it in his hand, not wanting his private parts to be seen.

Complete working English

Khallad ibn Yahya related to us. He said: Mis‘ar related to us. He said: Muharib ibn Dithar related to us, from Jabir ibn ‘Abd Allah, who said:

I came to the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, while he was in the mosque.

Mis‘ar said: I think he said, “In the forenoon.”

He said: “Pray two rak‘ahs.” He owed me a debt, and he repaid me and gave me extra.

Complete working English

Comparison findings

  • The selected unexpanded Abd Allah and all three construction stages remain. Mud bricks, palm fronds, palm wood, replacement pillars, carved stone, plaster and teak remain distinct. AI-assisted translation of the complete selected Arabic, compared with two visually inspected Arabic prints and two individually matched English comparison families. Omissions, shared references and wording variants remain documented. Independent Arabic, English, lexical, chapter and reference review is pending; this is not certification.

Complete working English

‘Ali ibn ‘Abd Allah related to us. He said: Ya‘qub ibn Ibrahim ibn Sa‘d related to us. He said: My father related to me, from Salih ibn Kaysan. He said: Nafi‘ related to us that ‘Abd Allah informed him:

In the time of the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, the mosque was built of mud bricks, its roof was palm fronds, and its pillars were palm wood. Abu Bakr did not add anything to it. ‘Umar added to it and built it according to its construction in the time of the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, with mud bricks and palm fronds, and made its pillars of wood again. Then ‘Uthman changed it, adding a great deal to it. He built its wall of carved stone and plaster, made its pillars of carved stone, and roofed it with teak.

Unchanged Arabic report

[435] حدثنا علي بن عبد الله قال: حدثنا يعقوب بن إبراهيم بن سعد قال: حدثني أبي عن صالح بن كيسان قال: حدثنا نافع أن عبد الله أخبره أن المسجد كان على عهد رسول الله ﷺ مبنيا باللبن وسقفه الجريد وعمده خشب النخل فلم يزد فيه أبو بكر شيئا وزاد فيه عمر وبناه على بنيانه في عهد رسول الله ﷺ باللبن والجريد وأعاد عمده خشبا ثم غيره عثمان فزاد فيه زيادة كثيرة وبنى جداره بالحجارة المنقوشة والقصة وجعل عمده من حجارة منقوشة وسقفه بالساج

Chapter heading and introduction

The construction of the mosque

The heading’s English is provisional. Any following introduction is preserved in Arabic; it is separate from the numbered hadith and its English translation remains pending.

باب بنيان المسجد

وقال أبو سعيد كان سقف المسجد من جريد النخل وأمر عمر ببناء المسجد، وقال أكن الناس من المطر وإياك أن تحمر أو تصفر فتفتن الناس، وقال أنس يتباهون بها ثم لا يعمرونها إلا قليلا، وقال ابن عباس لتزخرفنها كما زخرفت اليهود والنصارى

Hadith 436

AI-assisted working translation. Independent review pending.

Open this hadith →

Comparison findings

  • The whole introductory visit, cloak posture, one/two brick contrast and closing refuge statement remain. Both inspected Arabic prints explicitly contain تقتله الفئة الباغية. The selected The rebellious group will kill him is retained; the checked English EPUB omits that clause from its main quotation and supplies a parenthetical group identification instead. Bewley includes the clause.
  • Alas for provisionally renders the compassion expression ويح without inserting Allah into a clause where the source does not name Him. No names of later factions, battle, date or political interpretation are added. The complete Quranic chapter quotation remains separate browsing material.

AI-assisted translation of the complete selected Arabic, compared with two visually inspected Arabic prints and two individually matched English comparison families. Omissions, shared references and wording variants remain documented. Independent Arabic, English, lexical, chapter and reference review is pending; this is not certification.

Complete working English

Musaddad related to us. He said: ‘Abd al-‘Aziz ibn Mukhtar related to us. He said: Khalid al-Hadhdha’ related to us, from ‘Ikrimah:

Ibn ‘Abbas said to me and to his son ‘Ali: “Go to Abu Sa‘id and listen to some of his narrations.” We went and found him tending a walled garden. He took his cloak and sat with his knees drawn up and wrapped in it. Then he began relating to us until he came to mention the building of the mosque.

He said: We used to carry one brick at a time, while ‘Ammar carried two at a time. The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, saw him and brushed the dust off him, saying: “Alas for ‘Ammar! The rebellious group will kill him. He calls them to Paradise, and they call him to the Fire.” He said: ‘Ammar would say: “I seek refuge in Allah from tribulations.”

Complete working English

Khallad related to us. He said: ‘Abd al-Wahid ibn Ayman related to us, from his father, from Jabir:

A woman said: “Messenger of Allah, shall I have something made for you to sit on? I have a slave who is a carpenter.” He said: “If you wish.” So she had the pulpit made.

Complete working English

Yahya ibn Sulayman related to us. Ibn Wahb related to me. ‘Amr informed me that Bukayr related to him that ‘Asim ibn ‘Umar ibn Qatadah related to him that he heard ‘Ubayd Allah al-Khawlani, who heard ‘Uthman ibn ‘Affan say:

When people were speaking about him as he built the mosque of the Messenger, may Allah bless him and grant him peace: “You have said a great deal. I heard the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, say: ‘Whoever builds a mosque’—

Bukayr said: I think he said, “Seeking by it the face of Allah”—

‘Allah will build its like for him in Paradise.’”

Complete working English

Musa ibn Isma‘il related to us. He said: ‘Abd al-Wahid related to us. He said: Abu Burdah ibn ‘Abd Allah related to us. He said: I heard Abu Burdah, from his father, from the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, who said:

Complete working English

Abu al-Yaman al-Hakam ibn Nafi‘ related to us. He said: Shu‘ayb informed us, from al-Zuhri. He said: Abu Salamah ibn ‘Abd al-Rahman ibn ‘Awf informed me that he heard Hassan ibn Thabit al-Ansari ask Abu Hurayrah to bear witness:

Complete working English

‘Abd al-‘Aziz ibn ‘Abd Allah related to us. He said: Ibrahim ibn Sa‘d related to us, from Salih, from Ibn Shihab. He said: ‘Urwah ibn al-Zubayr informed me that ‘A’ishah said:

One day I saw the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, at the door of my room while the Abyssinians were playing in the mosque. The Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, was screening me with his cloak as I watched their play.

Ibrahim ibn al-Mundhir added: Ibn Wahb related to us. Yunus informed me, from Ibn Shihab, from ‘Urwah, from ‘A’ishah, who said:

I saw the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, while the Abyssinians were playing with their spears.

Hadith 444

AI-assisted working translation. Independent review pending.

Open this hadith →

Comparison findings

  • Both Sufyan once-said variants and the complete closing Ali/Yahya/Abd al-Wahhab, Ja’far and Malik transmissions remain in source order. The historical print places the Malik note before Ali’s note and includes extra wording after Amrah; the modern print follows the selected order and shorter Ali note. No missing text is invented for the selected abbreviated closing attributions.
  • The selected third-person opening and later first-person I mentioned are retained. Wala’ remains the technical term rather than being reduced to inheritance alone. The who sets free wording follows the Arabic لمن أعتق; the checked EPUB incorrectly says the manumitted. The complete condition statement retains a hundred times, not an inserted a hundred conditions. No legal ruling is supplied outside the source report.

AI-assisted translation of the complete selected Arabic, compared with two visually inspected Arabic prints and two individually matched English comparison families. Omissions, shared references and wording variants remain documented. Independent Arabic, English, lexical, chapter and reference review is pending; this is not certification.

Complete working English

‘Ali ibn ‘Abd Allah related to us. He said: Sufyan related to us, from Yahya, from ‘Amrah, from ‘A’ishah, who said:

Barirah came to her asking for help with her contract of emancipation. She said: “If you wish, I will pay your owners, and the wala’ will belong to me.” Her owners said: “If you wish, you can give her what remains.”

Sufyan once said: “If you wish, you can set her free, and the wala’ will belong to us.”

When the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, came, I mentioned that to him. He said: “Buy her and set her free, for the wala’ belongs to the one who sets free.” Then the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, stood on the pulpit.

Sufyan once said: “The Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, ascended the pulpit.”

He said: “What is the matter with people who stipulate conditions that are not in the Book of Allah? Whoever stipulates a condition that is not in the Book of Allah has no right to it, even if he stipulates it a hundred times.”

‘Ali said: Yahya and ‘Abd al-Wahhab said, from Yahya, from ‘Amrah. Ja‘far ibn ‘Awn said, from Yahya: “I heard ‘Amrah say: ‘I heard ‘A’ishah.’” Malik related it from Yahya, from ‘Amrah: “That Barirah…” He did not mention “ascended the pulpit.”

Unchanged Arabic report

[444] حدثنا علي بن عبد الله قال: حدثنا سفيان عن يحيى عن عمرة عن عائشة قالت أتتها بريرة تسألها في كتابتها فقالت إن شئت أعطيت أهلك ويكون الولاء لي، وقال أهلها إن شئت أعطيتها ما بقي، وقال سفيان مرة إن شئت أعتقتها ويكون الولاء لنا فلما جاء رسول الله ﷺ ذكرته ذلك، فقال ابتاعيها فأعتقيها فإن الولاء لمن أعتق ثم قام رسول الله ﷺ على المنبر، وقال سفيان مرة فصعد رسول الله ﷺ على المنبر، فقال: ما بال أقوام يشترطون شروطا ليست في كتاب الله من اشترط شرطا ليس في كتاب الله فليس له وإن اشترط مائة مرة قال علي قال يحيى وعبد الوهاب عن يحيى عن عمرة، وقال جعفر بن عون عن يحيى قال: سمعت عمرة قالت سمعت عائشة رواه مالك عن يحيى عن عمرة أن بريرة ولم يذكر صعد المنبر

Complete working English

‘Abd Allah ibn Muhammad related to us. He said: ‘Uthman ibn ‘Umar related to us. He said: Yunus informed us, from al-Zuhri, from ‘Abd Allah ibn Ka‘b ibn Malik, from Ka‘b:

He asked Ibn Abi Hadrad in the mosque to repay a debt he owed him. Their voices rose until the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, heard them while he was in his house. He came out to them and drew aside the curtain of his room. He called out: “Ka‘b!” He said: “At your service, Messenger of Allah.” He said: “Reduce this debt of yours by this much,” and gestured to him, meaning half. He said: “I have done so, Messenger of Allah.” He said: “Get up and repay him.”

Complete working English

Sulayman ibn Harb related to us. He said: Hammad ibn Zayd related to us, from Thabit, from Abu Rafi‘, from Abu Hurayrah:

A black man, or a black woman, used to sweep the mosque. He died, and the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, asked about him. They said: “He died.” He said: “Why did you not inform me about him? Show me his grave”—or he said, “her grave.” He came to her grave and prayed over her.

Complete working English

Ishaq ibn Ibrahim related to us. He said: Rawh and Muhammad ibn Ja‘far informed us, from Shu‘bah, from Muhammad ibn Ziyad, from Abu Hurayrah, from the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, who said:

or words like that—

“to interrupt my prayer. Allah gave me power over him, and I wanted to tie him to one of the pillars of the mosque so that, when morning came, all of you could look at him. Rawh said: He sent him away in disgrace.

Complete working English

‘Abd Allah ibn Yusuf related to us. He said: Al-Layth related to us. He said: Sa‘id ibn Abi Sa‘id related to us. He heard Abu Hurayrah say:

The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, sent horsemen toward Najd. They brought a man from Banu Hanifah called Thumamah ibn Uthal and tied him to one of the pillars of the mosque. The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, came out to him and said: “Release Thumamah.” He went to some palm trees near the mosque and washed himself. Then he entered the mosque and said: “I testify that there is no god but Allah and that Muhammad is the Messenger of Allah.”

Complete working English

Zakariyya ibn Yahya related to us. He said: ‘Abd Allah ibn Numayr related to us. He said: Hisham related to us, from his father, from ‘A’ishah, who said:

Sa‘d was wounded on the day of the Trench in the vein of his arm. The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, pitched a tent in the mosque so that he could visit him nearby. Nothing alarmed them—there was a tent of Banu Ghifar in the mosque—except blood flowing toward them. They said: “People of the tent, what is this coming to us from your direction?” There was Sa‘d, his wound streaming blood, and he died there.

Complete working English

‘Abd Allah ibn Yusuf related to us. He said: Malik informed us, from Muhammad ibn ‘Abd al-Rahman ibn Nawfal, from ‘Urwah, from Zaynab bint Abi Salamah, from Umm Salamah, who said:

I complained to the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, that I was ill. He said: “Perform tawaf behind the people while riding.” I performed tawaf while the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, was praying beside the House, reciting: “By the Mount, and a book inscribed.”

Comparison findings

  • This is a distinct complete numbered report with its own full Muhammad ibn al-Muthanna chain. The original two leading newlines are accounted for separately by the existing reviewed layout map; they do not join this narration to 452. Both inspected prints introduce an untitled chapter 79 here, absent from the selected digital source. The selected chapter grouping is retained and the missing printed heading is disclosed; no thematic title is invented.
  • The historical print names one companion and tentatively identifies the other. Those names are absent from the selected text and modern print and are not inserted. The two Prophet honorifics, two lamps comparison, separation and each man reaching his family remain. The English EPUB’s explanatory miracle wording and Bewley’s separate identifying note are not made part of the selected narration.

Complete working English

Muhammad ibn al-Muthanna related to us. He said: Mu‘adh ibn Hisham related to us. He said: My father related to me, from Qatadah. He said: Anas related to us:

Two men among the companions of the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, left the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, on a dark night. With them were what looked like two lamps, shining before them. When they parted, one went with each of them until he reached his family.
